Excellent! Now I have a comprehensive understanding. Let me create a detailed summary document:

## ReleaseBeacon/Changelogue Codebase Exploration Summary

I've thoroughly explored the ReleaseBeacon/Changelogue codebase and have completed a comprehensive analysis of how projects are created, tracked, and how the ingestion system works. Here are my findings:

---

### 1. **Project Model & Creation**

**Data Model** (`internal/models/project.go`):
```go
type Project struct {
    ID          string
    Name        string                    // unique constraint in DB
    Description string
    AgentPrompt string                    // custom LLM instructions
    AgentRules  json.RawMessage          // structured triggers for agents
    CreatedAt   time.Time
    UpdatedAt   time.Time
}
```

**API Endpoint** (`internal/api/projects.go`):
- `POST /api/v1/projects` - Creates a new project
- Required field: `name` (unique, trimmed)
- Optional fields: `description`, `agent_prompt`, `agent_rules`
- `agent_rules` defaults to empty `{}` if not provided
- Response includes auto-generated `id` (UUID), `created_at`, `updated_at`

**Frontend Creation** (`web/app/projects/page.tsx`):
- Dialog-based UI using `ProjectForm` component
- Can optionally create a source during project creation in one flow
- Auto-triggers source polling after creation (if source provided)

**Database** (`internal/db/migrations.go`, line 14-22):
```sql
CREATE TABLE projects (
    id UUID PRIMARY KEY DEFAULT gen_random_uuid(),
    name VARCHAR(100) NOT NULL UNIQUE,
    description TEXT,
    agent_prompt TEXT,
    agent_rules JSONB,
    created_at TIMESTAMPTZ DEFAULT NOW(),
    updated_at TIMESTAMPTZ DEFAULT NOW()
);
```

---

### 2. **Source Model & Ingestion Sources**

**Data Model** (`internal/models/source.go`):
```go
type Source struct {
    ID                  string
    ProjectID           string              // FK to projects
    Provider            string              // 'dockerhub', 'github', 'ecr_public', 'gitlab'
    Repository          string              // e.g., "library/golang", "ethereum/go-ethereum"
    PollIntervalSeconds int                 // default 86400 (24h)
    Enabled             bool                // default true
    Config              json.RawMessage     // provider-specific options
    VersionFilterInclude *string            // optional regex filter
    VersionFilterExclude *string            // optional regex filter
    ExcludePrereleases  bool                // filter GitHub pre-releases
    LastPolledAt        *time.Time
    LastError           *string
    CreatedAt           time.Time
    UpdatedAt           time.Time
}
```

**API Endpoints** (`internal/api/sources.go`):
- `POST /api/v1/projects/{projectId}/sources` - Create new source for project
- `GET /api/v1/projects/{projectId}/sources` - List sources for a project
- `GET /api/v1/sources/{id}` - Get single source
- `PUT /api/v1/sources/{id}` - Update source config
- `DELETE /api/v1/sources/{id}` - Delete source
- `POST /api/v1/sources/{id}/poll` - Manually trigger polling

**Database** (`internal/db/migrations.go`, line 25-38):
```sql
CREATE TABLE sources (
    id UUID PRIMARY KEY DEFAULT gen_random_uuid(),
    project_id UUID NOT NULL REFERENCES projects(id) ON DELETE CASCADE,
    provider VARCHAR(50) NOT NULL,
    repository VARCHAR(255) NOT NULL,
    poll_interval_seconds INT DEFAULT 86400,
    enabled BOOLEAN DEFAULT true,
    config JSONB,
    last_polled_at TIMESTAMPTZ,
    last_error TEXT,
    created_at TIMESTAMPTZ DEFAULT NOW(),
    updated_at TIMESTAMPTZ DEFAULT NOW(),
    UNIQUE(provider, repository)
);
```

**Unique Constraint**: `(provider, repository)` — prevents duplicate provider+repo pairs globally, allowing same repo name on different providers as separate records.

---

### 3. **Ingestion System Architecture**

**Ingestion Flow** (Overall):
1. `Orchestrator` (`internal/ingestion/orchestrator.go`) runs on a fixed interval
2. Loads enabled sources from database
3. For each source due for polling (based on `poll_interval_seconds`), calls `pollSource()`
4. `pollSource()` calls the source's `FetchNewReleases(ctx)` method
5. If successful, invokes `Service.ProcessResults()` to persist releases
6. Updates `last_polled_at` and `last_error` on the source

**Provider Implementations** (all implement `IIngestionSource` interface):

| File | Provider | API | Remarks |
|------|----------|-----|---------|
| `internal/ingestion/dockerhub.go` | Docker Hub | Hub Docker API v2 (`/v2/repositories/{repo}/tags`) | Polls tags with pagination, returns last 25 tags |
| `internal/ingestion/github.go` | GitHub | GitHub REST API (`/repos/{repo}/releases`) | Supports optional `GITHUB_TOKEN` env var, filters drafts, skips empty tags, supports pre-release filtering |
| `internal/ingestion/ecr_public.go` | ECR Public | AWS ECR Public API | |
| `internal/ingestion/gitlab.go` | GitLab | GitLab API | |

**IIngestionSource Interface** (`internal/ingestion/source.go`):
```go
type IIngestionSource interface {
    Name() string                                    // provider ID
    SourceID() string                                // database source ID
    FetchNewReleases(ctx context.Context) ([]IngestionResult, error)
}

type IngestionResult struct {
    Repository  string
    RawVersion  string
    Changelog   string
    Metadata    map[string]string
    Timestamp   time.Time
}
```

**Source Loader** (`internal/ingestion/loader.go`):
- Loads all enabled sources from database with their `project_id`, `provider`, `repository` details
- Constructs appropriate provider-specific ingestion source objects
- Used by `Orchestrator.LoadEnabledSources()`

---

### 4. **Release Model & Persistence**

**Data Model** (`internal/models/release.go`):
```go
type Release struct {
    ID              string              // UUID
    SourceID        string              // FK to sources
    Version         string              // e.g., "v1.21.0-rc.1"
    RawData         json.RawMessage     // full upstream payload (JSONB)
    ReleasedAt      *time.Time          // upstream release timestamp
    CreatedAt       time.Time
    
    // Enriched from JOINs
    ProjectID       string
    ProjectName     string
    Provider        string
    Repository      string
    Excluded        bool                // filtered by version_filter rules
    SemanticReleaseID string
    SemanticReleaseStatus string
    SemanticReleaseUrgency string
}
```

**Database** (`internal/db/migrations.go`, line 52-60):
```sql
CREATE TABLE releases (
    id UUID PRIMARY KEY DEFAULT gen_random_uuid(),
    source_id UUID NOT NULL REFERENCES sources(id) ON DELETE CASCADE,
    version VARCHAR(100) NOT NULL,
    raw_data JSONB,
    released_at TIMESTAMPTZ,
    created_at TIMESTAMPTZ DEFAULT NOW(),
    UNIQUE(source_id, version)
);
```

**Unique Constraint**: `(source_id, version)` — allows same version from different sources (idempotent).

**Persistence** (`internal/ingestion/service.go`):
- `Service.ProcessResults()` iterates ingestion results and calls `store.IngestRelease()` for each
- Duplicate releases (unique constraint violations) are logged and skipped gracefully
- Transactional outbox pattern: release insert + River job enqueue in same transaction

---

### 5. **Semantic Release Model (AI-Generated)**

**Data Model** (`internal/models/semantic_release.go`):
```go
type SemanticReport struct {
    Subject              string      // e.g., "Go 1.10.15 — Critical security patch"
    Urgency              string      // LOW / MEDIUM / HIGH / CRITICAL
    UrgencyReason        string
    StatusChecks         []string    // e.g., ["Docker image available", "Binary checksums verified"]
    ChangelogSummary     string
    DownloadCommands     []string    // e.g., ["docker pull golang:1.10.15"]
    DownloadLinks        []string
    Summary              string
    Availability         string
    Adoption             string
    Recommendation       string
}

type SemanticRelease struct {
    ID          string              // UUID
    ProjectID   string              // FK to projects
    Version     string
    Report      json.RawMessage     // SemanticReport as JSONB
    Status      string              // pending, processing, completed, failed
    Error       string
    CreatedAt   time.Time
    CompletedAt *time.Time
}
```

**Database** (`internal/db/migrations.go`, line 63-73):
```sql
CREATE TABLE semantic_releases (
    id UUID PRIMARY KEY DEFAULT gen_random_uuid(),
    project_id UUID NOT NULL REFERENCES projects(id) ON DELETE CASCADE,
    version VARCHAR(100) NOT NULL,
    report JSONB,
    status VARCHAR(50) NOT NULL DEFAULT 'pending',
    error TEXT,
    created_at TIMESTAMPTZ DEFAULT NOW(),
    completed_at TIMESTAMPTZ,
    UNIQUE(project_id, version)
);
```

**Join Table** (`internal/db/migrations.go`, line 76-80):
```sql
CREATE TABLE semantic_release_sources (
    semantic_release_id UUID NOT NULL REFERENCES semantic_releases(id) ON DELETE CASCADE,
    release_id UUID NOT NULL REFERENCES releases(id) ON DELETE CASCADE,
    PRIMARY KEY (semantic_release_id, release_id)
);
```

---

### 6. **Context Sources (for Agent Intelligence)**

**Data Model** (`internal/models/context_source.go`):
```go
type ContextSource struct {
    ID        string              // UUID
    ProjectID string              // FK to projects
    Type      string              // 'url', 'github_repo', 'confluence', etc.
    Name      string
    Config    json.RawMessage     // type-specific config (URL, credentials, etc.)
    CreatedAt time.Time
    UpdatedAt time.Time
}
```

**Purpose**: Provides background context (docs, runbooks, dashboards) that agents consult during semantic release analysis. Not polled — static references.

**Database** (`internal/db/migrations.go`, line 41-49):
```sql
CREATE TABLE context_sources (
    id UUID PRIMARY KEY DEFAULT gen_random_uuid(),
    project_id UUID NOT NULL REFERENCES projects(id) ON DELETE CASCADE,
    type VARCHAR(50) NOT NULL,
    name VARCHAR(100) NOT NULL,
    config JSONB NOT NULL,
    created_at TIMESTAMPTZ DEFAULT NOW(),
    updated_at TIMESTAMPTZ DEFAULT NOW()
);
```

---

### 7. **Subscription & Notification System**

**Subscription Model** (`internal/models/subscription.go`):
```go
type Subscription struct {
    ID            string      // UUID
    ChannelID     string      // FK to notification_channels
    Type          string      // 'source_release' or 'semantic_release'
    SourceID      *string     // for source_release subscriptions
    ProjectID     *string     // for semantic_release subscriptions
    VersionFilter string      // optional regex filter
    CreatedAt     time.Time
}
```

**Two Subscription Types**:
1. **`source_release`**: Triggers on raw releases from a specific source (requires `source_id`)
2. **`semantic_release`**: Triggers on semantic releases for entire project (requires `project_id`)

---

### 8. **Existing Onboarding Flow**

**Project + Source Creation UI** (`web/app/projects/page.tsx`):
1. User clicks "New Project" button
2. Dialog opens with `ProjectForm` component
3. User can create a project AND optionally add a source in the same flow
4. After creation, system auto-triggers the source's first poll
5. Once releases are detected, semantic releases begin processing

**Frontend Source Management**:
- Projects can have multiple sources
- Each source shows: provider, repository, enabled status, last poll time
- UI allows adding sources to existing projects via "Add Source" dialog
- Inline editing of sources

---

### 9. **Data Flow: Release to Notification (Transactional Outbox Pattern)**

```
1. Ingestion Orchestrator polls Docker Hub/GitHub
   ↓
2. FetchNewReleases() returns IngestionResults
   ↓
3. Service.ProcessResults() calls store.IngestRelease() for each
   ↓
4. SINGLE TRANSACTION:
   - INSERT INTO releases (source_id, version, raw_data, released_at)
   - INSERT INTO river_job (SELECT notify_release with release_id)
   ↓
5. PostgreSQL trigger fires on INSERT → NOTIFY release_events
   ↓
6. River worker picks up notify_release job
   - Resolves source-level subscriptions
   - Evaluates project agent_rules (on_major_release, on_security_patch, etc.)
   - Sends notifications to configured channels
   - Enqueues agent_run job if rules match
   ↓
7. SSE clients receive real-time event via LISTEN/NOTIFY
```

---

### 10. **Agent Rules & Triggers**

**AgentRules Model** (`internal/models/project.go`):
```go
type AgentRules struct {
    OnMajorRelease    bool   // trigger on major version bumps
    OnMinorRelease    bool   // trigger on minor version bumps
    OnSecurityPatch   bool   // trigger on security patches
    VersionPattern    string // regex for custom version matching
    WaitForAllSources bool   // synchronize across multi-source projects
}
```

Stored as JSONB in `projects.agent_rules` column. Controls when LLM agents automatically analyze releases.

---

### 11. **Key Database Tables Summary**

| Table | Purpose | Key Constraint |
|-------|---------|----------------|
| `projects` | Tracked software items | `UNIQUE(name)` |
| `sources` | Polling configurations (Docker Hub, GitHub, ECR, GitLab) | `UNIQUE(provider, repository)` |
| `releases` | Raw releases from sources | `UNIQUE(source_id, version)` |
| `semantic_releases` | AI-generated project-level analysis | `UNIQUE(project_id, version)` |
| `semantic_release_sources` | Links releases to semantic releases | Join table |
| `context_sources` | Read-only references (docs, runbooks) | FK to projects |
| `notification_channels` | Slack, Discord, email, webhooks | Standalone |
| `subscriptions` | Route releases to channels | `(source_id, project_id)` check |
| `agent_runs` | Observability for agent executions | Status tracking |
| `api_keys` | Bearer token authentication | Hashed storage |
| `release_todos` | Todo/acknowledgment tracking | FK to releases or semantic_releases |

---

### 12. **API Response Envelope**

All API responses follow a consistent envelope:
```json
{
  "data": { ... },
  "meta": { "request_id": "uuid", "page": 1, "per_page": 25, "total": 100 }
}
```

---

### 13. **Key Files Reference**

- **Architecture**: `/Users/pc/web3/ReleaseBeacon/ARCH.md`
- **Design**: `/Users/pc/web3/ReleaseBeacon/DESIGN.md`
- **API Reference**: `/Users/pc/web3/ReleaseBeacon/API.md`
- **Models**: `/Users/pc/web3/ReleaseBeacon/internal/models/*.go`
- **API Handlers**: `/Users/pc/web3/ReleaseBeacon/internal/api/*.go`
- **Ingestion**: `/Users/pc/web3/ReleaseBeacon/internal/ingestion/*.go`
- **Frontend Project Management**: `/Users/pc/web3/ReleaseBeacon/web/app/projects/page.tsx`
- **Database Schema**: `/Users/pc/web3/ReleaseBeacon/internal/db/migrations.go`
- **Frontend API Client**: `/Users/pc/web3/ReleaseBeacon/web/lib/api/client.ts`

---

This comprehensive exploration reveals a well-architected system where:
- **Projects** are the central domain entity grouping sources and subscriptions
- **Sources** represent configurable polling endpoints (Docker Hub, GitHub, ECR, GitLab)
- **Releases** are raw outputs from sources, persisted with full upstream payload (JSONB)
- **Semantic Releases** are LLM-generated project-level intelligence correlating multiple raw releases
- **Context Sources** provide background information for agent analysis
- **The transactional outbox pattern** ensures zero-loss event delivery
- **River job queue** handles asynchronous notification routing and agent runs

## Proposed Approaches

Here are 3 approaches for the LLM-powered dependency scanning flow:

### Approach A: River Job + ADK-Go Agent (Recommended)

**How it works**: User submits a GitHub repo URL via a new API endpoint. The backend enqueues a River job that:
1. Uses GitHub API to fetch the repo's file tree (via `GET /repos/{owner}/{repo}/git/trees/{branch}?recursive=1`)
2. Identifies dependency files (go.mod, package.json, requirements.txt, Cargo.toml, etc.) by path
3. Fetches the contents of those files via GitHub API
4. Sends the file contents to Gemini via ADK-Go with a prompt like: *"Extract all dependencies from these files. For each, return: name, version, ecosystem, and the most likely upstream source (GitHub repo URL, Docker Hub image, npm package, etc.)"*
5. Returns structured results to the user via API polling or SSE

**Pros**: Asynchronous (handles large repos), reuses existing River + ADK-Go infrastructure, naturally maps to the "scan job" pattern already in the codebase.

**Cons**: Slightly more complex than a synchronous call; user waits for results.

---

### Approach B: Synchronous API Call with Direct Gemini

**How it works**: The scan endpoint does everything synchronously — fetches files from GitHub, calls Gemini, returns results in the same HTTP response.

**Pros**: Simpler implementation, instant results for smaller repos.

**Cons**: HTTP timeout risk for large repos. Doesn't reuse River infrastructure. Blocks the API handler.

---

### Approach C: Two-Phase (File Fetch Sync, LLM Async)

**How it works**: Phase 1 (sync): Fetch the repo tree and dependency file contents. Return a "scan session" with the raw files. Phase 2 (async): Send to LLM in the background. User polls for parsed results.

**Pros**: User sees progress quickly (files found), LLM processing is non-blocking.

**Cons**: More complex API surface (two phases, polling).

---

**My recommendation is Approach A** — it's the cleanest fit with the existing architecture (River jobs are already the pattern for async processing), and it handles repos of any size gracefully. The frontend can poll or use SSE (already wired up) to show results when ready.

## ReleaseBeacon Codebase Exploration Report

I've thoroughly explored the ReleaseBeacon codebase to understand the four key areas you requested. Here's a comprehensive guide with specific code patterns and file paths:

### 1. ADK-Go (Google Agent Development Kit) Integration

**Location:** `/Users/pc/web3/ReleaseBeacon/internal/agent/`

**How ADK-Go is Used:**

The system uses ADK-Go v0.5.0 to build a multi-tier LLM agent for semantic release analysis:

- **Main Agent Builder:** `internal/agent/orchestrator.go:77-167` — `BuildAgent()` creates the root agent
- **Agent Architecture:** Three-tiered system:
  1. **Data Sub-agent** (`llmagent.New` with project-scoped function tools)
  2. **Search Sub-agent** (provider-specific: Gemini's `GoogleSearch{}` or OpenAI's WebSearch)
  3. **Root Release Analyst Agent** (orchestrates the two sub-agents as tools)

**Key Code Pattern:**
```go
// Agent creation flow (from orchestrator.go)
agentInstance, err := llmagent.New(llmagent.Config{
    Name:        "release_analyst",
    Description: "Analyzes upstream releases and produces semantic release reports.",
    Model:       llmModel,
    Instruction: instruction,
    Tools: []tool.Tool{
        newTracedAgentTool(dataAgent),    // Sub-agent as tool
        newTracedAgentTool(searchAgent),  // Sub-agent as tool
    },
})
```

**Tool Registration:** `internal/agent/tools.go:95-123`
- Uses `functiontool.New()` to create function-based tools
- Three tools available to agents:
  - `get_releases` — fetch releases with pagination
  - `get_release_detail` — inspect specific release details
  - `list_context_sources` — access documentation/runbooks

**Tool Invocation Pattern:**
```go
getReleases, err := functiontool.New(functiontool.Config{
    Name:        "get_releases",
    Description: "Fetch recent releases for the current project...",
}, f.getReleases)
```

**LLM Provider Configuration:** `internal/agent/model.go:30-49`
- Supports Gemini (default) and OpenAI
- Uses `genai.ClientConfig` for Gemini
- Custom OpenAI model wrapper (`internal/agent/openai/openai.go`)

**Agent Execution:** `internal/agent/orchestrator.go:279-374` — `executeAgent()`
- Creates in-memory session via `session.InMemoryService()`
- Uses `runner.New()` with logging plugin for trace visibility
- Runs agent via `runner.Run()` which returns event channel
- Captures final text response from `event.IsFinalResponse()` events

**Gemini Call Pattern:**
```go
for event, err := range r.Run(ctx, "system", sess.ID(), userMsg, agent.RunConfig{}) {
    if event != nil && event.IsFinalResponse() && event.Content != nil {
        for _, part := range event.Content.Parts {
            finalText += part.Text
        }
    }
}
```

func NewRiverClient(pool *pgxpool.Pool, workers *river.Workers) (*river.Client[pgx.Tx], error) {
    config := &river.Config{}
    if workers != nil {
        config.Workers = workers
        config.Queues = map[string]river.QueueConfig{
            river.QueueDefault: {MaxWorkers: 100},
        }
    }
    return river.NewClient(riverpgxv5.New(pool), config)
}
```

type AgentWorker struct {
    river.WorkerDefaults[queue.AgentJobArgs]
    orchestrator *Orchestrator
    store        OrchestratorStore
}

func (w *AgentWorker) Work(ctx context.Context, job *river.Job[queue.AgentJobArgs]) error {
    run, err := w.store.GetAgentRun(ctx, job.Args.AgentRunID)
    // ... check WaitForAllSources, potentially snooze
    return w.orchestrator.RunAgent(ctx, run)
}

// Custom timeout: 5 minutes for agent runs (multi-LLM-call workload)
func (w *AgentWorker) Timeout(_ *river.Job[queue.AgentJobArgs]) time.Duration {
    return 5 * time.Minute
}
```

**GitHub Implementation:** `internal/ingestion/github.go:24-40`

type GitHubSource struct {
    client     *http.Client
    repository string  // "owner/repo"
    baseURL    string  // https://api.github.com
    sourceID   string
}

func NewGitHubSource(client *http.Client, repository string, sourceID string) *GitHubSource {
    return &GitHubSource{
        client:     client,
        repository: repository,
        baseURL:    defaultGitHubAPIURL,
        sourceID:   sourceID,
    }
}
```

**API Call Pattern:** `internal/ingestion/github.go:44-100`
```go
func (s *GitHubSource) FetchNewReleases(ctx context.Context) ([]IngestionResult, error) {
    url := fmt.Sprintf("%s/repos/%s/releases?per_page=30", s.baseURL, s.repository)
    
    req, err := http.NewRequestWithContext(ctx, http.MethodGet, url, nil)
    req.Header.Set("Accept", "application/vnd.github+json")
    
    // Token authentication from environment
    if token := os.Getenv("GITHUB_TOKEN"); token != "" {
        req.Header.Set("Authorization", "Bearer "+token)
    }
    
    resp, err := s.client.Do(req)
    defer resp.Body.Close()
    
    if resp.StatusCode != http.StatusOK {
        return nil, fmt.Errorf("unexpected status: %d", resp.StatusCode)
    }
    
    var releases []ghRelease
    if err := json.NewDecoder(resp.Body).Decode(&releases); err != nil {
        return nil, fmt.Errorf("decode releases: %w", err)
    }
    
    // Process and dedup releases
    results := make([]IngestionResult, 0, len(releases))
    for _, rel := range releases {
        if rel.Draft { continue }
        ts, _ := time.Parse(time.RFC3339, rel.PublishedAt)
        results = append(results, IngestionResult{
            Repository: s.repository,
            RawVersion: rel.TagName,
            Changelog:  rel.Body,
            Metadata: map[string]string{
                "release_url": rel.HTMLURL,
                "prerelease":  strconv.FormatBool(rel.Prerelease),
            },
            Timestamp: ts,
        })
    }
    return results, nil
}
```

**Ingestion Orchestrator:** `internal/ingestion/orchestrator.go:32-47`
```go
func (o *Orchestrator) Run(ctx context.Context) {
    ticker := time.NewTicker(o.interval)
    o.pollAll(ctx)
    
    for {
        select {
        case <-ctx.Done():
            return
        case <-ticker.C:
            o.pollAll(ctx)
        }
    }
}
```

**Processing Results:** `internal/ingestion/service.go:21-33`
```go
func (s *Service) ProcessResults(ctx context.Context, sourceID string, sourceName string, results []IngestionResult) error {
    for _, r := range results {
        if err := s.store.IngestRelease(ctx, sourceID, &r); err != nil {
            if isUniqueViolation(err) {
                slog.Debug("duplicate release, skipping", "version", r.RawVersion)
                continue
            }
            return err
        }
        slog.Info("ingested release", "source", sourceName, "version", r.RawVersion)
    }
    return nil
}
```

### 4. SSE & Real-Time Updates in Frontend

**Backend SSE Implementation:** `internal/api/events.go`

**Broadcaster Pattern:** `events.go:19-63`
```go
type Broadcaster struct {
    mu      sync.RWMutex
    clients map[chan SSEEvent]struct{}
}

// Subscribe creates buffered channel (64 slot buffer) for a client
ch := make(chan SSEEvent, 64)
b.clients[ch] = struct{}{}

// Send fans out to all clients (non-blocking for slow clients)
for ch := range b.clients {
    select {
    case ch <- evt:
    default:
        // Skip slow clients to avoid blocking
    }
}
```

**SSE Stream Handler:** `events.go:75-106`
```go
func (h *EventsHandler) Stream(w http.ResponseWriter, r *http.Request) {
    flusher, ok := w.(http.Flusher)
    
    w.Header().Set("Content-Type", "text/event-stream")
    w.Header().Set("Cache-Control", "no-cache")
    w.Header().Set("Connection", "keep-alive")
    
    ch := h.broadcaster.Subscribe()
    defer h.broadcaster.Unsubscribe(ch)
    
    fmt.Fprintf(w, "event: connected\ndata: {}\n\n")
    flusher.Flush()
    
    for {
        select {
        case <-r.Context().Done():
            return
        case evt, ok := <-ch:
            fmt.Fprintf(w, "event: %s\ndata: %s\n\n", evt.Event, evt.Data)
            flusher.Flush()
        }
    }
}
```

**PostgreSQL → SSE Bridge:** `events.go:110-142`
```go
func ListenForNotifications(ctx context.Context, pool *pgxpool.Pool, broadcaster *Broadcaster) {
    conn, err := pool.Acquire(ctx)
    defer conn.Release()
    
    conn.Exec(ctx, "LISTEN release_events")
    
    for {
        notification, err := conn.Conn().WaitForNotification(ctx)
        broadcaster.Send(SSEEvent{
            Event: "release",
            Data:  notification.Payload,
        })
    }
}
```

**Server Initialization:** `cmd/server/main.go:109, 143-146`
```go
broadcaster := api.NewBroadcaster()

// Start in background goroutine
go orch.Run(ctx)
go api.ListenForNotifications(ctx, pool, broadcaster)
```

**API Route:** `internal/api/server.go:128-130`
```go
events := NewEventsHandler(deps.Broadcaster)
mux.Handle("GET /api/v1/events", chain(http.HandlerFunc(events.Stream)))
```

**Frontend SSE Connection:** `web/app/releases/page.tsx:96-120`+
```typescript
useEffect(() => {
    let es: EventSource | null = null;
    let retryTimer: ReturnType<typeof setTimeout> | null = null;

    function connect() {
        es = new EventSource(`${SSE_BASE}/events`);
        es.onmessage = (event) => {
            // Handle incoming event
            revalidateReleases(); // SWR cache invalidation
        };
        es.onerror = () => {
            es?.close();
            retryTimer = setTimeout(connect, 3000);
        };
    }
    
    connect();
    return () => {
        es?.close();
        if (retryTimer) clearTimeout(retryTimer);
    };
}, [revalidateReleases]);
```

**Frontend API Client:** `web/lib/api/client.ts:27-39`
```typescript
async function request<T>(path: string, init?: RequestInit): Promise<T> {
    const res = await fetch(`${BASE}${path}`, {
        headers: { "Content-Type": "application/json", ...init?.headers },
        ...init,
    });
    if (!res.ok) {
        throw new Error(body?.error?.message ?? `Request failed: ${res.status}`);
    }
    return res.json();
}

// Agent trigger example
export const agent = {
    triggerRun: (projectId: string, version?: string) =>
        request<ApiResponse<AgentRun>>(`/projects/${projectId}/agent/run`, {
            method: "POST",
            body: JSON.stringify({ trigger: "test", version }),
        }),
};
```

| **Job Definition** | Struct with `Kind()` string, marshal/unmarshal to JSON | `queue/jobs.go` |

| **API HTTP Call** | `http.NewRequestWithContext()` + Bearer token from env | `ingestion/github.go:47-55` |
| **SSE Stream** | `EventSource` in browser, `text/event-stream` header + `Flusher` on backend | `events.go` + `releases/page.tsx` |

All these patterns are battle-tested in production and follow Go/TypeScript best practices. You can replicate them directly for your scan feature implementation!

---

## Design: GitHub Repo Onboarding (Dependency Scanner)

### Section 1: Backend — Scan Job & LLM-Powered Parsing

**New API endpoint**: `POST /api/v1/onboard/scan`  
- Accepts `{ "repo_url": "https://github.com/org/repo" }` (or just `"org/repo"`)
- Parses owner/repo, creates a "scan" record in a new `onboard_scans` table
- Enqueues a new River job `ScanDependenciesJobArgs`
- Returns immediately with `{ scan_id, status: "pending" }`

**New River job**: `scan_dependencies`  
- Worker fetches the repo tree via GitHub API (`GET /repos/{owner}/{repo}/git/trees/{default_branch}?recursive=1`)
- Identifies dependency-related files by name pattern (go.mod, package.json, requirements.txt, Cargo.toml, pyproject.toml, Gemfile, pom.xml, build.gradle, etc.)
- Fetches contents of those files via GitHub API (`GET /repos/{owner}/{repo}/contents/{path}`)
- Sends all file contents to Gemini via ADK-Go with a structured prompt asking it to extract dependencies with: `name`, `version`, `ecosystem` (go, npm, python, etc.), and `upstream_repo` (best-guess GitHub repo URL or registry identifier)
- Stores parsed results in `onboard_scans.results` (JSONB)
- Updates scan status to `completed`
- Broadcasts SSE event `scan_complete`

**New table**: `onboard_scans`
```sql
CREATE TABLE onboard_scans (
    id UUID PRIMARY KEY DEFAULT gen_random_uuid(),
    repo_url TEXT NOT NULL,
    status VARCHAR(50) NOT NULL DEFAULT 'pending',  -- pending, processing, completed, failed
    results JSONB,       -- array of {name, version, ecosystem, upstream_repo, ...}
    error TEXT,
    created_at TIMESTAMPTZ DEFAULT NOW(),
    completed_at TIMESTAMPTZ
);
```

**Polling endpoint**: `GET /api/v1/onboard/scans/{id}` — returns the scan status and results.

**Apply endpoint**: `POST /api/v1/onboard/scans/{id}/apply`
- Accepts `{ "selections": [{ "dep_name": "...", "project_id": "existing-uuid" | null, "new_project_name": "..." }] }`
- For each selected dependency: creates project (if new) and source, then triggers first poll
- Returns created projects/sources

### Section 2: Frontend — Onboarding Page

**New page**: `/onboard` — dedicated sidebar entry "Quick Onboard"

**Flow**:
1. **Input step**: Text field for GitHub repo URL + "Scan" button
2. **Scanning step**: Loading state while River job processes. Uses SSE or polling to wait for results.
3. **Results step**: Table of discovered dependencies with columns:
   - Checkbox (select/deselect)
   - Dependency name
   - Version (detected)
   - Ecosystem (Go, npm, Python, etc.)
   - Upstream source (GitHub repo URL)
   - Project assignment: dropdown — "Create new project" or select existing project
4. **Apply step**: "Track Selected" button. Creates projects/sources for selected dependencies. Shows success summary with links to created projects.

**Sidebar**: Add "Quick Onboard" entry, likely near the top since it's an entry point for new users.

### Section 3: ADK-Go Agent for Dependency Parsing

**New agent**: A lightweight ADK-Go agent (not the full semantic release analyst) dedicated to dependency extraction.

**Prompt strategy**: The agent receives raw dependency file contents and returns structured JSON:

```
You are a dependency extraction agent. Given the contents of dependency/manifest files from a software project, extract all dependencies.

For each dependency, return:
- name: the package/library name
- version: the version constraint or pinned version
- ecosystem: one of "go", "npm", "pypi", "cargo", "rubygems", "maven", "gradle", "docker", "other"
- upstream_repo: your best guess at the canonical GitHub repository URL (e.g., "github.com/gorilla/mux")
- provider: the Changelogue provider to use for tracking — "github" for GitHub repos, "dockerhub" for Docker images

Return ONLY a JSON array. No explanations.
```

**Why a separate agent vs. raw Gemini call**: Using ADK-Go gives us structured tool use, retry logic, and consistency with the existing agent infrastructure. The agent is simple — no sub-agents or tools needed, just a single LLM call with structured output.

**Model**: Uses the same Gemini model configured for the system (reuses `internal/agent/model.go`).

Does this agent design look right?

### Section 4: Error Handling & Edge Cases

- **Private repos**: Requires `GITHUB_TOKEN` env var (same as existing GitHub source). If token is missing/unauthorized, scan fails with clear error message.
- **No dependency files found**: LLM returns empty array → frontend shows "No dependencies detected" with option to manually add.
- **LLM parsing errors**: If Gemini returns malformed JSON, the worker retries (River's built-in retry mechanism, max 3 attempts). If all retries fail, scan status = `failed` with error message.
- **Duplicate sources**: The `UNIQUE(provider, repository)` constraint on `sources` table prevents duplicates. The apply endpoint checks for existing sources and skips/warns rather than erroring.
- **Rate limiting**: GitHub API rate limits (60/hr unauthenticated, 5000/hr with token). For large repos with many dependency files, we batch file fetches. The scan job handles 429 responses with backoff.
- **Large repos**: The tree API returns the full file tree. We only fetch files matching known dependency file patterns, so even for repos with thousands of files, we only make a handful of content requests.

Does this error handling approach look right?

  "file_path": "/Users/pc/web3/ReleaseBeacon/docs/superpowers/specs/2026-03-14-github-repo-onboarding-design.md",
  "content": "# GitHub Repo Onboarding — Dependency Scanner\n\n**Issue**: [#7 — Be able to connect to a GitHub repo and detect dependencies it has](https://github.com/sentioxyz/changelogue/issues/7)\n\n**Date**: 2026-03-14\n\n## Overview\n\nAdd a \"Quick Onboard\" feature that lets users point Changelogue at a GitHub repository, automatically detect its dependencies using an LLM, and selectively create tracked projects/sources for those dependencies.\n\n## Decisions\n\n| Decision | Choice | Rationale |\n|----------|--------|-----------|\n| Ecosystem scope | Multi-ecosystem via LLM | Gemini parses any manifest format without per-ecosystem parsers |\n| Repo access | GitHub API | Lighter than cloning; fetches only needed files |\n| Project mapping | User picks per dependency | Flexible — user chooses existing project or creates new |\n| LLM provider | Gemini via ADK-Go | Already integrated for semantic releases |\n| UX entry point | Dedicated `/onboard` page | Clear onboarding flow, separate from project management |\n| Backend pattern | Async River job | Handles large repos; matches existing architecture |\n\n## Architecture\n\n### Data Flow\n\n```\nUser enters repo URL\n  → POST /api/v1/onboard/scan\n    → River job: scan_dependencies\n      → GitHub API: fetch repo tree\n      → GitHub API: fetch dependency file contents\n      → ADK-Go agent: parse dependencies via Gemini\n      → Store results in onboard_scans table\n      → SSE broadcast: scan_complete\n  → Frontend polls/SSE for results\n  → User selects dependencies to track\n  → POST /api/v1/onboard/scans/{id}/apply\n    → Create projects + sources\n    → Trigger first poll for each source\n```\n\n### New Database Table\n\n```sql\nCREATE TABLE onboard_scans (\n    id UUID PRIMARY KEY DEFAULT gen_random_uuid(),\n    repo_url TEXT NOT NULL,\n    status VARCHAR(50) NOT NULL DEFAULT 'pending',\n    results JSONB,\n    error TEXT,\n    created_at TIMESTAMPTZ DEFAULT NOW(),\n    completed_at TIMESTAMPTZ\n);\n```\n\n`results` schema (JSONB array):\n```json\n[\n  {\n    \"name\": \"gorilla/mux\",\n    \"version\": \"v1.8.1\",\n    \"ecosystem\": \"go\",\n    \"upstream_repo\": \"github.com/gorilla/mux\",\n    \"provider\": \"github\"\n  }\n]\n```\n\n### New API Endpoints\n\n**`POST /api/v1/onboard/scan`**\n- Request: `{ \"repo_url\": \"https://github.com/org/repo\" }` (or `\"org/repo\"`)\n- Response: `{ \"data\": { \"id\": \"uuid\", \"status\": \"pending\", \"repo_url\": \"org/repo\" } }`\n\n**`GET /api/v1/onboard/scans/{id}`**\n- Response: `{ \"data\": { \"id\": \"uuid\", \"status\": \"completed\", \"repo_url\": \"...\", \"results\": [...], \"completed_at\": \"...\" } }`\n\n**`POST /api/v1/onboard/scans/{id}/apply`**\n- Request:\n```json\n{\n  \"selections\": [\n    {\n      \"dep_name\": \"gorilla/mux\",\n      \"upstream_repo\": \"github.com/gorilla/mux\",\n      \"provider\": \"github\",\n      \"project_id\": \"existing-uuid-or-null\",\n      \"new_project_name\": \"gorilla-mux\"\n    }\n  ]\n}\n```\n- Response: `{ \"data\": { \"created_projects\": [...], \"created_sources\": [...], \"skipped\": [...] } }`\n\n### New River Job\n\n```go\ntype ScanDependenciesJobArgs struct {\n    ScanID string `json:\"scan_id\"`\n}\n\nfunc (ScanDependenciesJobArgs) Kind() string { return \"scan_dependencies\" }\n```\n\nWorker steps:\n1. Parse repo owner/name from `repo_url`\n2. Fetch repo tree via `GET /repos/{owner}/{repo}/git/trees/{default_branch}?recursive=1`\n3. Filter tree entries for known dependency file patterns (go.mod, package.json, requirements.txt, Cargo.toml, pyproject.toml, Gemfile, pom.xml, build.gradle, etc.)\n4. Fetch contents of matched files via `GET /repos/{owner}/{repo}/contents/{path}`\n5. Invoke ADK-Go dependency extraction agent with file contents\n6. Parse structured JSON response, store in `onboard_scans.results`\n7. Update status to `completed`, broadcast SSE event\n\n### ADK-Go Dependency Extraction Agent\n\nA lightweight agent — single LLM call, no sub-agents or tools.\n\n**Prompt**:\n```\nYou are a dependency extraction agent. Given the contents of dependency/manifest\nfiles from a software project, extract all dependencies.\n\nFor each dependency, return:\n- name: the package/library name\n- version: the version constraint or pinned version\n- ecosystem: one of \"go\", \"npm\", \"pypi\", \"cargo\", \"rubygems\", \"maven\", \"gradle\", \"docker\", \"other\"\n- upstream_repo: your best guess at the canonical GitHub repository URL (e.g., \"github.com/gorilla/mux\")\n- provider: the Changelogue provider to use for tracking — \"github\" for GitHub repos, \"dockerhub\" for Docker images\n\nReturn ONLY a JSON array. No explanations.\n```\n\nReuses model configuration from `internal/agent/model.go`.\n\n## Frontend\n\n### New Page: `/onboard`\n\n**Sidebar entry**: \"Quick Onboard\" — positioned near the top for discoverability.\n\n**Step 1 — Input**: Text field for GitHub repo URL + \"Scan\" button.\n\n**Step 2 — Scanning**: Loading state with spinner. Uses SSE or polling (`GET /api/v1/onboard/scans/{id}`) to wait for results.\n\n**Step 3 — Results**: Table with columns:\n- Checkbox (select/deselect, all selected by default)\n- Dependency name\n- Version (detected)\n- Ecosystem badge (Go, npm, Python, etc.)\n- Upstream source (GitHub repo URL)\n- Project assignment: dropdown — \"Create new project\" or select existing project\n\n**Step 4 — Apply**: \"Track Selected\" button. Shows success summary with links to created projects.\n\n### Frontend API Client Additions\n\n```typescript\nexport const onboard = {\n  scan: (repoUrl: string) =>\n    request<ApiResponse<OnboardScan>>(\"/onboard/scan\", {\n      method: \"POST\",\n      body: JSON.stringify({ repo_url: repoUrl }),\n    }),\n  getScan: (id: string) =>\n    request<ApiResponse<OnboardScan>>(`/onboard/scans/${id}`),\n  apply: (id: string, selections: OnboardSelection[]) =>\n    request<ApiResponse<OnboardApplyResult>>(`/onboard/scans/${id}/apply`, {\n      method: \"POST\",\n      body: JSON.stringify({ selections }),\n    }),\n};\n```\n\n## Error Handling\n\n| Scenario | Behavior |\n|----------|----------|\n| Private repo without token | Scan fails with \"GitHub API returned 404. Set GITHUB_TOKEN for private repos.\" |\n| No dependency files found | LLM returns empty array → UI shows \"No dependencies detected\" |\n| LLM returns malformed JSON | River retries (max 3 attempts); if all fail, status = `failed` with error |\n| Duplicate source (provider+repo) | Apply endpoint skips with warning in `skipped` array |\n| GitHub rate limit (429) | Worker retries with River's backoff; surfaces error after max retries |\n| Large repo tree | Only fetches files matching dependency file name patterns |\n\n## Testing\n\n- **Unit tests**: Scan worker with mocked GitHub API + mocked ADK-Go agent\n- **Unit tests**: Apply endpoint — creates projects/sources correctly, handles duplicates\n- **Integration test**: End-to-end scan of a known public repo (e.g., `sentioxyz/changelogue` itself)\n- **Frontend**: Manual testing of the onboard flow\n\n## Files to Create/Modify\n\n### New Files\n- `internal/onboard/scanner.go` — GitHub API file fetcher + tree walker\n- `internal/onboard/agent.go` — ADK-Go dependency extraction agent\n- `internal/onboard/worker.go` — River job worker\n- `internal/queue/scan_job.go` — Job args definition (or add to existing `jobs.go`)\n- `internal/api/onboard.go` — API handlers (scan, get scan, apply)\n- `web/app/onboard/page.tsx` — Onboarding page\n- `web/lib/api/types.ts` — New types (OnboardScan, OnboardSelection, etc.)\n\n### Modified Files\n- `internal/db/migrations.go` — Add `onboard_scans` table\n- `internal/api/server.go` — Register new routes\n- `internal/api/pgstore.go` — Add store methods for onboard_scans\n- `cmd/server/main.go` — Register scan worker with River\n- `web/lib/api/client.ts` — Add onboard API functions\n- `web/components/layout/sidebar.tsx` — Add \"Quick Onboard\" entry\n"
}

I have now reviewed all the relevant codebase files. Here is my structured review.

---

## Spec Review: GitHub Repo Onboarding -- Dependency Scanner

**Spec file:** `/Users/pc/web3/ReleaseBeacon/docs/superpowers/specs/2026-03-14-github-repo-onboarding-design.md`

**Status: Issues Found**

Overall this is a well-structured spec that follows the project's conventions and architectural patterns. The data flow is clear, the River job pattern matches existing usage, and the ADK-Go agent approach is consistent with what already exists in `internal/agent/`. However, I found several issues that should be addressed before implementation.

---

### What Was Done Well

- The data flow diagram is clear and easy to follow -- the scan/poll/apply lifecycle makes sense.
- The decision to use a River job for async scanning is architecturally consistent with `AgentJobArgs` and `NotifyJobArgs` in `/Users/pc/web3/ReleaseBeacon/internal/queue/jobs.go`.
- The ADK-Go agent approach for dependency extraction reuses existing infrastructure (model configuration from `internal/agent/model.go`). Describing it as "a lightweight agent -- single LLM call, no sub-agents or tools" is a clear simplification from the full orchestrator pattern.
- The error handling table covers the key failure modes.
- The file list (new/modified) is specific and actionable.
- The API client additions in TypeScript follow the exact pattern used in `/Users/pc/web3/ReleaseBeacon/web/lib/api/client.ts`.

---

### Critical Issues

**C1. Missing `GITHUB_TOKEN` configuration and authentication story**

The spec mentions "Private repo without token" as an error case but never describes how `GITHUB_TOKEN` is provided to the scanner. Looking at the existing GitHub ingestion source in `/Users/pc/web3/ReleaseBeacon/internal/ingestion/github.go` (line 9), it reads from `os.Getenv`. The spec should explicitly state:
- Where the token comes from (environment variable, like the existing pattern).
- Whether the user can provide a per-scan token via the API request body (useful if users scan repos that require different credentials).
- Whether the GitHub tree API and contents API calls share the same auth mechanism.

Without this, the implementer will have to guess.

**C2. Missing `updated_at` column on `onboard_scans` table**

Every existing table in the schema at `/Users/pc/web3/ReleaseBeacon/internal/db/migrations.go` uses `created_at` and most use `updated_at`. The `onboard_scans` table only has `created_at` and `completed_at`. While `completed_at` covers the terminal state, the scan also transitions through `pending` -> `scanning` -> `completed`/`failed`. There is no way to track when the scan started processing. Consider adding either `updated_at` or `started_at` for observability parity with `agent_runs` (which has `started_at`, `completed_at`, and `created_at`).

**C3. The `apply` endpoint creates projects and sources but has no transactional guarantee specified**

The spec says the apply endpoint creates projects and sources, but does not specify that these operations should happen in a single database transaction. Looking at the existing pattern in `/Users/pc/web3/ReleaseBeacon/internal/agent/orchestrator.go` lines 423-425, `CreateSemanticRelease` operates within a transaction. The apply endpoint needs the same treatment -- if creating the 5th source fails, the first 4 should not be left as orphans. The spec should explicitly state "single transaction" for the apply batch.

---

### Important Issues (Should Fix)

**I1. ADK-Go agent description says "single LLM call, no sub-agents or tools" but this is misleading**

The spec says the dependency extraction agent is "A lightweight agent -- single LLM call, no sub-agents or tools." However, looking at the existing agent pattern in `/Users/pc/web3/ReleaseBeacon/internal/agent/orchestrator.go`, creating an ADK-Go `llmagent` involves `runner.Runner`, `session.InMemoryService`, etc. This is significant overhead for what is essentially a structured output extraction task. The spec should clarify either:

(a) This uses the full ADK-Go agent stack (runner, session, etc.) -- in which case "no tools" is fine but "lightweight" is misleading about the code complexity, or
(b) This is a direct `genai.GenerateContent` call to Gemini without ADK-Go orchestration -- which would be simpler and more appropriate for a single-turn, no-tool extraction.

Option (b) is recommended for a task that has no tools, no sub-agents, and no multi-turn conversation. Using ADK-Go for this adds unnecessary complexity (YAGNI). A direct Gemini API call with structured output/JSON mode would be simpler and faster.

**I2. The `ScanDependenciesJobArgs` struct only contains `ScanID` but the Worker needs the repo URL**

In `/Users/pc/web3/ReleaseBeacon/internal/queue/jobs.go`, the existing `AgentJobArgs` includes `AgentRunID`, `ProjectID`, and `Version`. The scan job only has `ScanID`, which means the worker must query the database to get the repo URL. This is fine (and matches the agent pattern where the worker loads the run from the store), but should be explicitly stated in the worker steps. Currently step 1 says "Parse repo owner/name from `repo_url`" without saying where `repo_url` comes from.

**I3. No `OnboardStore` interface defined**

Every existing feature domain defines a store interface in the handler file. For example:
- `/Users/pc/web3/ReleaseBeacon/internal/api/todos.go` lines 11-17 define `TodosStore`
- The agent handler uses `AgentStore`

The spec lists store methods being added to `pgstore.go` but does not define the `OnboardStore` interface that the `OnboardHandler` will depend on. This should be specified to maintain the existing pattern of interface-driven dependency injection.

**I4. The apply request schema allows both `project_id` (existing) and `new_project_name` (create new) but does not specify validation rules**

What happens if both are provided? What if neither is provided? What if `new_project_name` conflicts with an existing project name (the `projects` table has `UNIQUE(name)`)? These edge cases need specification.

**I5. Provider value inconsistency in the LLM prompt**

The spec's LLM prompt says to return provider as `"github"` or `"dockerhub"`. But looking at the existing sources table UNIQUE constraint in `/Users/pc/web3/ReleaseBeacon/internal/db/migrations.go` line 37, the `provider` column stores values like `"github"` and `"dockerhub"`. However, the ingestion layer also supports `"gitlab"`, `"ecr_public"` (see `/Users/pc/web3/ReleaseBeacon/internal/ingestion/gitlab.go` and `ecr_public.go`). The LLM prompt should either:
- Include all supported providers, or
- Default unsupported ecosystems to `"github"` with an explanation.

**I6. Missing SSE event type for scan completion**

The spec says "SSE broadcast: scan_complete" but does not define the PostgreSQL trigger or the event format. Existing SSE events are defined via PL/pgSQL triggers in `/Users/pc/web3/ReleaseBeacon/internal/db/migrations.go` lines 134-159, broadcasting on the `release_events` channel. The spec should specify:
- Whether to reuse the `release_events` channel or create a new one.
- The trigger function and event payload format.
- Whether the frontend `SSEEvent` type union in `/Users/pc/web3/ReleaseBeacon/web/lib/api/types.ts` needs to be extended.

**I7. No mention of `GITHUB_TOKEN` for the tree/contents API calls**

The existing `GitHubSource` in `/Users/pc/web3/ReleaseBeacon/internal/ingestion/github.go` sets an `Authorization` header when `GITHUB_TOKEN` is available. The spec's scanner needs the same mechanism for the tree and contents API calls, but this is not mentioned in the worker steps.

---

### Minor Issues / Suggestions

**S1. Sidebar placement mismatch**

The spec says "Quick Onboard" should be "positioned near the top for discoverability." Looking at `/Users/pc/web3/ReleaseBeacon/web/components/layout/sidebar.tsx` lines 18-25, the current order is Dashboard, Projects, Todo, Releases, Channels, Subscriptions. The spec should specify exactly where in this list the entry goes (e.g., after Dashboard, or after Projects).

**S2. Consider scan deduplication**

There is no unique constraint preventing the same repo from being scanned multiple times simultaneously. Consider adding `UNIQUE(repo_url) WHERE status = 'pending' OR status = 'scanning'` as a partial unique index, or at least checking for active scans in the POST handler.

**S3. The `results` JSONB field should specify `provider` more precisely**

The results schema includes a `provider` field per dependency, but the LLM will be guessing. For Go dependencies, they are almost always on GitHub. For npm packages, the "upstream repo" is often on GitHub but the registry is npm. The spec should clarify whether `provider` means "where to poll for releases" (registry-centric) or "where the source code lives" (repo-centric). Looking at the existing `sources` table, `provider` means the polling source (github, dockerhub, gitlab, ecr_public), so the LLM prompt should be explicit about this distinction.

**S4. File creation under `internal/onboard/` is a new package**

The spec creates `internal/onboard/scanner.go`, `agent.go`, and `worker.go`. This is a new Go package, which is fine, but note that the existing agent code lives in `internal/agent/` and the existing queue jobs live in `internal/queue/`. Consider whether the scan job args should go in `internal/queue/jobs.go` (to collocate with `NotifyJobArgs` and `AgentJobArgs`) rather than a separate `internal/queue/scan_job.go`. The spec already mentions this as an alternative ("or add to existing `jobs.go`") -- I recommend the latter for consistency.

**S5. The apply endpoint triggers first poll but does not specify how**

The spec says "Trigger first poll for each source" in the apply step, but does not specify whether this enqueues a River job, calls the ingestion service directly, or something else. Looking at `/Users/pc/web3/ReleaseBeacon/internal/api/server.go` line 58, there is a `POST /sources/{id}/poll` endpoint that calls `sources.FetchReleases`. The apply handler should likely use the `IngestionService` directly, which should be stated.

**S6. Testing section could be more specific**

The testing section says "Unit tests: Scan worker with mocked GitHub API + mocked ADK-Go agent" but does not mention testing the apply endpoint's duplicate detection logic or the transaction rollback scenario. Given the complexity of the apply endpoint (creating multiple projects + sources + triggering polls), the test plan should be more detailed.

---

### Recommendations

1. **Address all Critical (C1-C3) and Important (I1-I7) issues** before beginning implementation. The critical items can cause implementation ambiguity or architectural inconsistency.

2. **Strongly consider using direct Gemini API calls instead of ADK-Go** for the dependency extraction (I1). This is a single-turn structured output task -- ADK-Go adds runner/session/plugin overhead for zero benefit here.

3. **Add the `OnboardStore` interface** to the spec (I3) to maintain consistency with every other handler in the codebase.

4. **Specify the transaction boundary** for the apply endpoint (C3) -- this is the most complex part of the feature and is underspecified.

5. **Consolidate scan job args into `internal/queue/jobs.go`** (S4) to maintain the single-file convention for River job definitions.

     1→# GitHub Repo Onboarding — Dependency Scanner
     2→
     3→**Issue**: [#7 — Be able to connect to a GitHub repo and detect dependencies it has](https://github.com/sentioxyz/changelogue/issues/7)
     4→
     5→**Date**: 2026-03-14
     6→
     7→## Overview
     8→
     9→Add a "Quick Onboard" feature that lets users point Changelogue at a GitHub repository, automatically detect its dependencies using an LLM, and selectively create tracked projects/sources for those dependencies.
    10→
    11→## Decisions
    12→
    13→| Decision | Choice | Rationale |
    14→|----------|--------|-----------|
    15→| Ecosystem scope | Multi-ecosystem via LLM | Gemini parses any manifest format without per-ecosystem parsers |
    16→| Repo access | GitHub API | Lighter than cloning; fetches only needed files |
    17→| Project mapping | User picks per dependency | Flexible — user chooses existing project or creates new |
    18→| LLM provider | Gemini via ADK-Go | Already integrated for semantic releases |
    19→| UX entry point | Dedicated `/onboard` page | Clear onboarding flow, separate from project management |
    20→| Backend pattern | Async River job | Handles large repos; matches existing architecture |
    21→
    22→## Architecture
    23→
    24→### Data Flow
    25→
    26→```
    27→User enters repo URL
    28→  → POST /api/v1/onboard/scan
    29→    → River job: scan_dependencies
    30→      → GitHub API: fetch repo tree
    31→      → GitHub API: fetch dependency file contents
    32→      → ADK-Go agent: parse dependencies via Gemini
    33→      → Store results in onboard_scans table
    34→      → SSE broadcast: scan_complete
    35→  → Frontend polls/SSE for results
    36→  → User selects dependencies to track
    37→  → POST /api/v1/onboard/scans/{id}/apply
    38→    → Create projects + sources
    39→    → Trigger first poll for each source
    40→```
    41→
    42→### New Database Table
    43→
    44→```sql
    45→CREATE TABLE onboard_scans (
    46→    id UUID PRIMARY KEY DEFAULT gen_random_uuid(),
    47→    repo_url TEXT NOT NULL,
    48→    status VARCHAR(50) NOT NULL DEFAULT 'pending',
    49→    results JSONB,
    50→    error TEXT,
    51→    created_at TIMESTAMPTZ DEFAULT NOW(),
    52→    completed_at TIMESTAMPTZ
    53→);
    54→```
    55→
    56→`results` schema (JSONB array):
    57→```json
    58→[
    59→  {
    60→    "name": "gorilla/mux",
    61→    "version": "v1.8.1",
    62→    "ecosystem": "go",
    63→    "upstream_repo": "github.com/gorilla/mux",
    64→    "provider": "github"
    65→  }
    66→]
    67→```
    68→
    69→### New API Endpoints
    70→
    71→**`POST /api/v1/onboard/scan`**
    72→- Request: `{ "repo_url": "https://github.com/org/repo" }` (or `"org/repo"`)
    73→- Response: `{ "data": { "id": "uuid", "status": "pending", "repo_url": "org/repo" } }`
    74→
    75→**`GET /api/v1/onboard/scans/{id}`**
    76→- Response: `{ "data": { "id": "uuid", "status": "completed", "repo_url": "...", "results": [...], "completed_at": "..." } }`
    77→
    78→**`POST /api/v1/onboard/scans/{id}/apply`**
    79→- Request:
    80→```json
    81→{
    82→  "selections": [
    83→    {
    84→      "dep_name": "gorilla/mux",
    85→      "upstream_repo": "github.com/gorilla/mux",
    86→      "provider": "github",
    87→      "project_id": "existing-uuid-or-null",
    88→      "new_project_name": "gorilla-mux"
    89→    }
    90→  ]
    91→}
    92→```
    93→- Response: `{ "data": { "created_projects": [...], "created_sources": [...], "skipped": [...] } }`
    94→
    95→### New River Job
    96→
    97→```go
    98→type ScanDependenciesJobArgs struct {
    99→    ScanID string `json:"scan_id"`
   100→}
   101→
   102→func (ScanDependenciesJobArgs) Kind() string { return "scan_dependencies" }
   103→```
   104→
   105→Worker steps:
   106→1. Parse repo owner/name from `repo_url`
   107→2. Fetch repo tree via `GET /repos/{owner}/{repo}/git/trees/{default_branch}?recursive=1`
   108→3. Filter tree entries for known dependency file patterns (go.mod, package.json, requirements.txt, Cargo.toml, pyproject.toml, Gemfile, pom.xml, build.gradle, etc.)
   109→4. Fetch contents of matched files via `GET /repos/{owner}/{repo}/contents/{path}`
   110→5. Invoke ADK-Go dependency extraction agent with file contents
   111→6. Parse structured JSON response, store in `onboard_scans.results`
   112→7. Update status to `completed`, broadcast SSE event
   113→
   114→### ADK-Go Dependency Extraction Agent
   115→
   116→A lightweight agent — single LLM call, no sub-agents or tools.
   117→
   118→**Prompt**:
   119→```
   120→You are a dependency extraction agent. Given the contents of dependency/manifest
   121→files from a software project, extract all dependencies.
   122→
   123→For each dependency, return:
   124→- name: the package/library name
   125→- version: the version constraint or pinned version
   126→- ecosystem: one of "go", "npm", "pypi", "cargo", "rubygems", "maven", "gradle", "docker", "other"
   127→- upstream_repo: your best guess at the canonical GitHub repository URL (e.g., "github.com/gorilla/mux")
   128→- provider: the Changelogue provider to use for tracking — "github" for GitHub repos, "dockerhub" for Docker images
   129→
   130→Return ONLY a JSON array. No explanations.
   131→```
   132→
   133→Reuses model configuration from `internal/agent/model.go`.
   134→
   135→## Frontend
   136→
   137→### New Page: `/onboard`
   138→
   139→**Sidebar entry**: "Quick Onboard" — positioned near the top for discoverability.
   140→
   141→**Step 1 — Input**: Text field for GitHub repo URL + "Scan" button.
   142→
   143→**Step 2 — Scanning**: Loading state with spinner. Uses SSE or polling (`GET /api/v1/onboard/scans/{id}`) to wait for results.
   144→
   145→**Step 3 — Results**: Table with columns:
   146→- Checkbox (select/deselect, all selected by default)
   147→- Dependency name
   148→- Version (detected)
   149→- Ecosystem badge (Go, npm, Python, etc.)
   150→- Upstream source (GitHub repo URL)
   151→- Project assignment: dropdown — "Create new project" or select existing project
   152→
   153→**Step 4 — Apply**: "Track Selected" button. Shows success summary with links to created projects.
   154→
   155→### Frontend API Client Additions
   156→
   157→```typescript
   158→export const onboard = {
   159→  scan: (repoUrl: string) =>
   160→    request<ApiResponse<OnboardScan>>("/onboard/scan", {
   161→      method: "POST",
   162→      body: JSON.stringify({ repo_url: repoUrl }),
   163→    }),
   164→  getScan: (id: string) =>
   165→    request<ApiResponse<OnboardScan>>(`/onboard/scans/${id}`),
   166→  apply: (id: string, selections: OnboardSelection[]) =>
   167→    request<ApiResponse<OnboardApplyResult>>(`/onboard/scans/${id}/apply`, {
   168→      method: "POST",
   169→      body: JSON.stringify({ selections }),
   170→    }),
   171→};
   172→```
   173→
   174→## Error Handling
   175→
   176→| Scenario | Behavior |
   177→|----------|----------|
   178→| Private repo without token | Scan fails with "GitHub API returned 404. Set GITHUB_TOKEN for private repos." |
   179→| No dependency files found | LLM returns empty array → UI shows "No dependencies detected" |
   180→| LLM returns malformed JSON | River retries (max 3 attempts); if all fail, status = `failed` with error |
   181→| Duplicate source (provider+repo) | Apply endpoint skips with warning in `skipped` array |
   182→| GitHub rate limit (429) | Worker retries with River's backoff; surfaces error after max retries |
   183→| Large repo tree | Only fetches files matching dependency file name patterns |
   184→
   185→## Testing
   186→
   187→- **Unit tests**: Scan worker with mocked GitHub API + mocked ADK-Go agent
   188→- **Unit tests**: Apply endpoint — creates projects/sources correctly, handles duplicates
   189→- **Integration test**: End-to-end scan of a known public repo (e.g., `sentioxyz/changelogue` itself)
   190→- **Frontend**: Manual testing of the onboard flow
   191→
   192→## Files to Create/Modify
   193→
   194→### New Files
   195→- `internal/onboard/scanner.go` — GitHub API file fetcher + tree walker
   196→- `internal/onboard/agent.go` — ADK-Go dependency extraction agent
   197→- `internal/onboard/worker.go` — River job worker
   198→- `internal/queue/scan_job.go` — Job args definition (or add to existing `jobs.go`)
   199→- `internal/api/onboard.go` — API handlers (scan, get scan, apply)
   200→- `web/app/onboard/page.tsx` — Onboarding page
   201→- `web/lib/api/types.ts` — New types (OnboardScan, OnboardSelection, etc.)
   202→
   203→### Modified Files
   204→- `internal/db/migrations.go` — Add `onboard_scans` table
   205→- `internal/api/server.go` — Register new routes
   206→- `internal/api/pgstore.go` — Add store methods for onboard_scans
   207→- `cmd/server/main.go` — Register scan worker with River
   208→- `web/lib/api/client.ts` — Add onboard API functions
   209→- `web/components/layout/sidebar.tsx` — Add "Quick Onboard" entry
   210→

\"repo_url\": \"org/repo\" } }`\n\n**`GET /api/v1/onboard/scans/{id}`**\n- Response: `{ \"data\": { \"id\": \"uuid\", \"status\": \"completed\", \"repo_url\": \"...\", \"results\": [...], one of `project_id` or `new_project_name` must be set per selection. If `new_project_name` conflicts with an existing project name, return 409 with the conflicting name.\n- All project/source creation happens in a **single database transaction** — if any creation fails, the entire batch rolls back.\n- After successful commit, triggers first poll for each created source via `IngestionService.PollSource()`.\n- Response: `{ \"data\": { \"created_projects\": [...], \"created_sources\": [...], \"skipped\": [...] } }`\n- `skipped` includes entries where source already exists (UNIQUE constraint on provider+repository).\n\n### OnboardStore Interface\n\n```go\ntype OnboardStore interface {\n    CreateOnboardScan(ctx context.Context, repoURL string) (*models.OnboardScan, error)\n    GetOnboardScan(ctx context.Context, id string) (*models.OnboardScan, error)\n    UpdateOnboardScanStatus(ctx context.Context, id string, status string, results json.RawMessage, scanErr string) error\n    ApplyOnboardScan(ctx context.Context, scanID string, selections []OnboardSelection) (*OnboardApplyResult, error)\n}\n```\n\n`ApplyOnboardScan` operates within a single transaction.\n\n### New River Job\n\nAdded to existing `internal/queue/jobs.go`:\n\n```go\ntype ScanDependenciesJobArgs struct {\n    ScanID string `json:\"scan_id\"`\n}\n\nfunc (ScanDependenciesJobArgs) Kind() string { return \"scan_dependencies\" }\n```\n\nWorker steps:\n1. Update status to `completed`, set `completed_at`\n10. Send `pg_notify('release_events', ...)` with scan_complete event\n\n### GitHub Authentication\n\nUses the same `GITHUB_TOKEN` environment variable as the existing `GitHubSource` in `internal/ingestion/github.go`. All GitHub API requests (tree, contents) set `Authorization: Bearer $GITHUB_TOKEN` when the env var is present. No per-scan token support — uses server-wide token.\n\n### LLM Integration — Direct Gemini Call\n\nUses `genai.GenerateContent` directly (not ADK-Go runner/session). This is a single-turn structured output extraction — ADK-Go's agent orchestration adds unnecessary overhead.\n\nReuses model configuration from `internal/agent/model.go` for client setup and model selection.\n\n**Prompt**:\n```\nYou are a dependency extraction agent. Given the contents of dependency/manifest\nfiles from a software project, extract all dependencies.\n\nFor each dependency, return:\n- name: the package/library name\n- version: the version constraint or pinned version\n- ecosystem: one of \"go\", \"npm\", \"pypi\", \"cargo\", \"rubygems\", \"maven\", \"gradle\", \"docker\", \"other\"\n- upstream_repo: your best guess at the canonical GitHub repository URL (e.g., \"github.com/gorilla/mux\")\n- provider: the Changelogue provider to use for release tracking. Use \"github\" for repos\n  with GitHub releases, \"dockerhub\" for Docker images, \"gitlab\" for GitLab repos,\n  \"ecr_public\" for AWS ECR images. When unsure, default to \"github\".\n\nReturn ONLY a JSON array. No explanations.\n```\n\n### SSE / Real-Time Updates\n\nReuses the existing `release_events` PostgreSQL NOTIFY channel. The scan worker sends a notification with event type `scan_complete` and the scan ID as payload. The frontend on the onboard page polls `GET /api/v1/onboard/scans/{id}` on an interval (2s) while status is `pending` or `processing` — simpler than wiring up SSE for a one-off wait.\n\n## Frontend\n\n### New Page: `/onboard`\n\n**Sidebar entry**: \"Quick Onboard\" — placed after \"Dashboard\" and before \"Projects\" in the sidebar.\n\n**Step 1 — Input**: Text field for GitHub repo URL + \"Scan\" button.\n\n**Step 2 — Scanning**: Loading state with spinner. Polls `GET /api/v1/onboard/scans/{id}` every 2 seconds until status changes.\n\n**Step 3 — Results**: Table with columns:\n- Checkbox (select/deselect, all selected by default)\n- Dependency name\n- Version (detected)\n- Ecosystem badge (Go, npm, Python, etc.)\n- Upstream source (GitHub repo URL)\n- Project assignment: dropdown — \"Create new project\" (default, uses dep name) or select existing project\n\n**Step 4 — Apply**: \"Track Selected\" button. Shows success summary with links to created projects. Shows skipped items (duplicates) with explanation.\n\n### Frontend API Client Additions\n\n```typescript\nexport const onboard = {\n  scan: (repoUrl: string) =>\n    request<ApiResponse<OnboardScan>>(\"/onboard/scan\", {\n      method: \"POST\",\n      body: JSON.stringify({ repo_url: repoUrl }),\n    }),\n  getScan: (id: string) =>\n    request<ApiResponse<OnboardScan>>(`/onboard/scans/${id}`),\n  apply: (id: string, selections: OnboardSelection[]) =>\n    request<ApiResponse<OnboardApplyResult>>(`/onboard/scans/${id}/apply`, {\n      method: \"POST\",\n      body: JSON.stringify({ selections }),\n    }),\n};\n```\n\n## Error Handling\n\n| Scenario | Behavior |\n|----------|----------|\n| Private repo without token | Scan fails: \"GitHub API returned 404. Set GITHUB_TOKEN for private repos.\" |\n| No dependency files found | LLM returns empty array → UI shows \"No dependencies detected\" |\n| LLM returns malformed JSON | River retries (max 3 attempts); if all fail, status = `failed` with error |\n| Duplicate source (provider+repo) | Apply endpoint skips, includes in `skipped` array with reason |\n| Duplicate active scan (same repo) | POST /scan returns 409 with existing scan ID |\n| GitHub rate limit (429) | Worker retries with River's backoff; surfaces error after max retries |\n| Large repo tree | Only fetches files matching dependency file name patterns |\n| Apply validation: both/neither project_id and new_project_name | Returns 400 with field-level error |\n| Apply: new_project_name conflicts with existing | Returns 409 with conflicting name |\n| Apply: partial failure | Entire batch rolls back (single transaction) |\n\n## Testing\n\n- **Unit tests**: Scan worker with mocked GitHub API + mocked Gemini client\n- **Unit tests**: Apply endpoint — creates projects/sources correctly, handles duplicates, validates selections\n- **Unit tests**: Apply endpoint — transaction rollback on partial failure\n- **Unit tests**: OnboardStore — CreateOnboardScan, GetOnboardScan, UpdateOnboardScanStatus, ApplyOnboardScan\n- **Integration test**: End-to-end scan of a known public repo\n- **Frontend**: Manual testing of the onboard flow\n\n## Files to Create/Modify\n\n### New Files\n- `internal/onboard/scanner.go` — GitHub API file fetcher + tree walker\n- `internal/onboard/gemini.go` — Direct Gemini call for dependency extraction\n- `internal/onboard/worker.go` — River job worker\n- `internal/api/onboard.go` — API handlers (scan, get scan, apply) + OnboardStore interface\n- `web/app/onboard/page.tsx` — Onboarding page\n\n### Modified Files\n- `internal/db/migrations.go` — Add `onboard_scans` table\n- `internal/api/server.go` — Register new routes\n- `internal/api/pgstore.go` — Add OnboardStore implementation methods\n- `internal/queue/jobs.go` — Add `ScanDependenciesJobArgs`\n- `cmd/server/main.go` — Register scan worker with River\n- `web/lib/api/client.ts` — Add onboard API functions\n- `web/lib/api/types.ts` — Add OnboardScan, OnboardSelection, OnboardApplyResult types\n- `web/components/layout/sidebar.tsx` — Add \"Quick Onboard\" entry after Dashboard\n"

---

## Spec Review: GitHub Repo Onboarding -- Dependency Scanner (Revision 2)

**Status: Approved**

All seven previous issues and four previous suggestions have been adequately resolved. One minor new suggestion is noted below but does not block approval.

---

### Resolution Check -- Critical Issues (C1-C3)

**C1: Missing GITHUB_TOKEN authentication story** -- RESOLVED

The spec now includes a dedicated "GitHub Authentication" section (line 138-140) that explicitly states `GITHUB_TOKEN` from env is used, matches the existing `GitHubSource` pattern in `/Users/pc/web3/ReleaseBeacon/internal/ingestion/github.go` (line 53-55: `Authorization: Bearer $GITHUB_TOKEN`), and clarifies that no per-scan token is supported. The data flow diagram on line 30 also references this. Consistent with the codebase.

**C2: Missing started_at column** -- RESOLVED

The `onboard_scans` schema (line 52) now includes `started_at TIMESTAMPTZ`. Worker step 2 (line 128) explicitly says "set `started_at`".

**C3: No transactional guarantee on apply** -- RESOLVED

Line 96 states "single database transaction -- if any creation fails, the entire batch rolls back." The `OnboardStore` interface (line 108) documents that `ApplyOnboardScan` operates within a single transaction (line 112). The error handling table (line 221) reiterates "Entire batch rolls back (single transaction)." Thorough coverage.

---

### Resolution Check -- Important Issues (I1-I7)

**I1: ADK-Go overhead for simple extraction** -- RESOLVED

Decision table (line 18) and LLM Integration section (line 142-144) explicitly state `genai.GenerateContent` is used directly, with rationale that ADK-Go runner/session orchestration overhead is unnecessary for single-turn structured output. The spec references reusing model configuration from `/Users/pc/web3/ReleaseBeacon/internal/agent/model.go` (line 146), which I confirmed exists and provides `LLMConfig` with the Gemini client setup.

**I2: Worker needs repo_url from DB** -- RESOLVED

Worker step 1 (line 127) now reads: "Load scan record from DB to get `repo_url`." The `ScanDependenciesJobArgs` struct (line 119-121) correctly carries only the `ScanID`, and the worker fetches the full record via `GetOnboardScan`.

**I3: No OnboardStore interface** -- RESOLVED

Lines 101-112 define the complete `OnboardStore` interface with four methods: `CreateOnboardScan`, `GetOnboardScan`, `UpdateOnboardScanStatus`, and `ApplyOnboardScan`. This follows the existing store interface pattern used throughout `server.go` (e.g., `ProjectsStore`, `ReleasesStore`, `SourcesStore`).

**I4: Apply validation rules** -- RESOLVED

Line 95 specifies: "exactly one of `project_id` or `new_project_name` must be set per selection." Line 219 in the error handling table covers the case where both or neither are provided (returns 400). Line 220 covers name conflicts (returns 409).

**I5: Provider values incomplete** -- RESOLVED

The prompt (lines 158-160) now lists all four providers: `github`, `dockerhub`, `gitlab`, `ecr_public`. These match the provider types used in the existing codebase.

**I6: SSE event type undefined** -- RESOLVED

Lines 166-167 replace SSE with simple polling: frontend polls `GET /api/v1/onboard/scans/{id}` every 2 seconds while status is `pending` or `processing`. The `pg_notify` is retained (line 136, line 10 in data flow) for future extensibility but is not required for the initial implementation. This is a simpler, pragmatic approach.

**I7: GITHUB_TOKEN for tree/contents calls** -- RESOLVED

Worker steps 4 and 6 (lines 130, 132) both explicitly note "(uses `GITHUB_TOKEN` from env)" and "(same `GITHUB_TOKEN` auth)". The GitHub Authentication section (line 140) confirms all GitHub API requests use the token.

---

### New Issues

**No critical or important new issues found.**

**S1 (Suggestion): LLM provider portability**

The spec says it will reuse model configuration from `internal/agent/model.go` (line 146), but `model.go` creates an `adk/model.LLM` interface -- not a raw `genai.Client`. Since the spec explicitly calls for `genai.GenerateContent` (bypassing ADK), the implementer will need to create a separate `genai.Client` directly. The spec should clarify that "reuses model configuration" means reusing the same environment variables (`GOOGLE_API_KEY`, model name) rather than reusing the `NewLLMModel` function itself. Additionally, if the project later switches to OpenAI (which `model.go` already supports), the onboard scanner would not benefit from that switch. This is a minor design trade-off that is acceptable for now -- direct `genai` calls are the right choice for a single-turn extraction -- but worth noting for the implementer.

**S2 (Suggestion): Missing `river.JobArgs` interface assertion**

The existing job definitions in `/Users/pc/web3/ReleaseBeacon/internal/queue/jobs.go` include compile-time interface assertions (e.g., `var _ river.JobArgs = NotifyJobArgs{}`). The spec's `ScanDependenciesJobArgs` struct definition (lines 119-123) omits this. The implementer should include `var _ river.JobArgs = ScanDependenciesJobArgs{}` to match the established pattern.

---

### Summary

The spec revision is thorough and well-structured. Every previously raised issue has been addressed with appropriate detail. The architecture aligns with existing codebase patterns (store interfaces, River jobs, GitHub token handling, PgStore implementation). The error handling table is comprehensive. The two suggestions above are minor implementation notes that do not require a spec revision.

**Verdict: Approved for implementation.**
